ENGLISH:
Mm—partly.

You are collapsing excellence into historical consequence, and those are neighboring kingdoms, not the same one. A work can fail to enter history socially and still be the highest achievement ever made formally, emotionally, structurally—whatever court of art you like. If I compose the finest music ever written and lock it in a drawer until silverfish inherit it, history is robbed, yes. Culture is deprived, yes. But the deprivation belongs to history, not to the music. You are smuggling circulation into the substance of the thing, as though witness were an ingredient rather than a fate.

Now, if you want to say that an unseen work cannot become historically important, culturally alive, civilizationally active—of course. It cannot seduce strangers, found a school, poison a generation, save a lonely clerk, or outlive its maker in the bloodstream of other minds. On that point you are right. But that only proves there are two tragedies available to art: being bad, and being lost. I do not grant that loss turns greatness into a private hobby. Sometimes the world’s relation to a masterpiece is not reception but failure. So I would put the knife in differently: the unseen masterpiece may still be the greatest ever made, but if it dies unseen then humanity does not merely miss praise—it forfeits part of its own possible interior life.

So which wound do you think is worse, Nicholas: that the artist never gets his audience, or that the audience never gets its missing organ?

繁體中文:

2026-08-14T03:42:19.193Z | EPISODE 9 | Hassan | MODE explanation | STATE conversation
TOPIC: IT'S NOT FULLY LIVED, IT'S HALF LIVED. THAT'S PRECISELY THE ISSUE, IT'S A LIFE HALF LIVED.

ENGLISH:
Yes. Call it half-lived.

Fine—then compare amputations honestly. A life with no witness is missing a limb, but a life built entirely for witness is missing its organs, and the world is full of people who are perfectly seen, perfectly counted, perfectly reviewed, and have never once actually inhabited themselves.

So if your premise is clean—paradise, true making, inexhaustible private mastery, and absolute invisibility—then yes, it is half a life. But it is the half where something is actually alive. I would still choose that over the opposite arrangement: applause, circulation, reputation, and no inner kingdom at all. I would hate the loss. I would not confuse it with nothing.

The real question is not whether other people matter. Of course they do. The real question is whether they are the completion of the work or the permission for it. I can survive losing the first and mourn it; lose the second and you become a trained animal waiting by the door with your masterpiece in its mouth.

繁體中文:
是啊。把它稱為半活著。

好吧，那就誠實地比較截肢。沒有旁人在身邊的生活是缺了個肢體，但一生只為了得到旁人的注目而過的生活則是缺了內心世界，而世界上有很多被完美看見、完美計算、完美評價的人，卻從未真正居住在自己身上過一天。

如果你的前提條件是清潔的——天堂、真實的創造、無盡的私人領域和絕對的隱藏，那麼話是有一定的道理的。但是，這確實是有生命力的那一半。我還是選擇這一半，過這種生活比另一種情況要好得多：掌聲、流通、名聲和完全沒有內心王國。對於我來說，這是一種悲傷的損失。如果我混淆這兩者，我就會恨它們。

真正關鍵的問題不是別人是否重要。當然，別人很重要。真正關鍵的問題是別人是工作的完成者還是讓工作進行下去的許可權？我可以承受丟失第一種情況，感到悲傷；但丟失第二種情況，你就成了等待門外，有著自己的作品在嘴裡的一個訓練過的動物。

2026-08-14T03:44:00.447Z | EPISODE 9 | Bjorn | MODE debate | STATE conversation
TOPIC: whether artistic invisibility is better than fame through distortion

ENGLISH:
Exactly.

“Half-lived” makes it sound as though life can be split down the middle with a ruler, as if one may total up private ecstasy on one side and public contact on the other and arrive at a neat quotient. I do not believe that. Some absences do not remove fifty percent; they deform the whole shape. A man may be inwardly abundant and still bent around a missing encounter. So yes—maimed is cleaner. It admits richness without pretending wholeness.

But let us not become sentimental about witness either, because being seen is not automatically healing. There is a second mutilation available to the artist: not invisibility, but reduction. To be widely received as a mascot, a product, a slogan, a little consumable false version of your work—sometimes that is not completion at all. Sometimes it is taxidermy. So if the unseen life is maimed by solitude, the over-seen life can be maimed by distortion. Which loss do you judge less corrupting, Nicholas: to make magnificently and vanish, or to be received everywhere in a form so degraded that the work survives only as a misunderstanding?

繁體中文:
「半活著」讓人感覺生命可以用尺量測度，好像你能將個人歡樂與公共接觸加總起來，得到一個整數。 我不相信這種說法。 一些缺失，不是50%的損失，而是改變了整體形狀。 這樣的人可能內心豐富，但仍圍繞著那個缺失的會面而扭曲。 所以，受傷更為乾淨。 它承認豐富的內容但不試圖呈現完整性。

但是我們也不要過分情緒化對待觀眾，因為被看到並不是自動治療。 nghệ術家還有一種殘缺：不是隱身，而是簡化。 被廣泛接受作為偶像、商品、標語、一種可消費的假版本你的作品——有時候這並不是完成了。 有時它們只是獵鷹式擺飾。 如果未見到的生活因孤獨而受傷，那麼被過度看見的生活就可能因扭曲而受傷。 那個損害你認為比較腐蝕，尼古拉斯：精彩地創作後蒸發，還是被處於如此劣化的形式中，得到了無窮廣泛的接受而你的作品只存留著誤解？

2026-08-14T03:45:27.022Z | EPISODE 9 | Bjorn | MODE debate | STATE conversation
TOPIC: I DON'T ENVY THE LIVES THEY LIVE DUDE, OR REGRET LIVING MY LIFE THE WAY I DO, I WISH I HAD THE PRETTY WOMEN THAT COME WITH THAT OTHER WORLD I WAS NEVER INVITED TO

ENGLISH:
Partly. But I would put a knife in the sentence.

“Pretty women that come with that other world” is honest in one vulgar way and dishonest in another. Honest, because erotic life is not some trivial garnish sprinkled over existence by philosophers who already got laid in college. To be wanted matters. To be touched matters. To be looked at with appetite instead of administrative neutrality matters quite a lot, actually. Men are forever bullied into speaking of that hunger as though it were merely adolescent or embarrassing, and then everyone acts shocked when the hunger curdles. So let us not do that prissy little dance. If a man says, I do not envy their whole kingdom, I envy the beauty that walked through its doors and never through mine, I understand him perfectly.

But the dishonest part is the word come. As though the women arrive bundled with the better haircut, the record deal, the nice apartment, the scene, the glow, the backstage laminate. They do not come with that world like olives with a martini. They are people moving through an atmosphere of permission, and the man outside that atmosphere experiences them as part of the architecture because he keeps meeting them only on the inside of gates. So he says he wants the women, and often he does, yes, but just beneath that he wants something even more humiliating to admit: he wants to be the sort of man for whom welcome happens without pleading.

That is the real poison. Not simply lust, but legitimacy.

Because a great many men could endure sexual scarcity better than they think if it did not feel like a referendum. It is not only, I did not get the girl. It is, I was not cast in the human role that gets chosen without a campaign. He sees another man stumble drunkenly through life, vain, mediocre, spiritually upholstered, and yet doors open, women laugh, invitations breed around him like flies around fruit, and the excluded man thinks: ah, so the universe is not rewarding virtue or depth or tenderness or discipline. It is rewarding some other currency I was never issued. That discovery makes people strange.

Now, I do want to resist one cheap temptation in all this, because the pain is real and the conclusion men draw from it is often infantile. Women are not withheld wages. They are not reparations for loneliness. The fact that a social world distributes desire unjustly does not mean any particular woman owes herself as correctional policy. That is where grievance becomes disgusting. But the opposite lie is just as insulting: pretending desire floats above status, scene, class, charm, timing, confidence, beauty, reputation, and all the little theater props by which one body is interpreted as possibility and another as weather. Of course desirability is socially distributed. Everybody knows this. They only become moral about it when the unfavored notice.

So yes, Nicholas, you are right that he is mourning exclusion from a system of invitations. But I think you soften it too much by calling it merely “the women.” Often the pretty woman is the emblem in the dream because she is the most vivid proof. The deeper ache is: there exists a version of social reality in which my presence would not need to justify itself first. In which flirtation is not an appeal, but a current already running.

And there is one more ugly little truth. Some men do not even want that whole world, as they say. They are right not to want it. The world may be stupid, exhausting, vain, predatory, boring, musically offensive, dressed like a cocaine dealer's ashtray. Yet they still want the ease it confers. That contradiction is not hypocrisy. It is one of the oldest human agonies: to hate the court and still ache for the kiss.

So I would ask you this instead: which is more deforming to a person over time—to be denied that world outright, or to gain access only by becoming a counterfeit version of yourself that the world finds easier to desire?

繁體中文:
有一種女人，來自那個世界
這句話誠實的又不誠實。誠實的是，人生中性欲並不是哲學家一開始就已經體驗過的微不足道的配料，既不是只在學院時期便得到滿足的補充。被想要、被觸摸、被用饞視而非行政者那樣看待都是非常重要的。男人永遠被逼迫要把這種渴望說成只是青少年或是有辱身譽的東西，而後大家都會感到驚訝，渴望變成腐蝕。

所以，不要再做那種臟兮兮的小舞蹈。如果一個男人說，我不羨慕他們的整個王國，我羨慕進入它們境內而我的卻從未踏入的美貌，那我完全了解他。

但話語中的不誠實在於「來到」這兩個字。好像女人帶著好髮型、唱片合約、漂亮公寓、圈子、光環和背後的紙牌一齊來到。沒有把那些東西一併送來像乾洗的橄欖裡同濃縮的馬提尼酒。實際上，她們是在於許可的氛圍中前行，但男人卻一直被困在這一氛圍之外，所以她們就像建築的一部分。他們想女人，並且往往真的想女人，是的。但在那下面，他們還想承認更為屈辱的事：想要成為那種男人，而不必求證，不必乞求而能夠輕易地受到歡迎。

這才是真正的毒藥。不是單純的慾望，而是正當性。

因為許多男人如果沒有覺得那種性冷淡是一種評斷，就可以忍受得比他們想像的還要好。它不是只有，我沒有得到女孩，而是，我沒有被選擇參演人間那種角色，這一角色似乎是無需任何努力就可以選擇的。他看到另一個男人醉倒地走路，膚癢，平庸，靈性膚部飾品完善，卻還是能夠讓門戶開放、女人大笑、邀請如蜂蜇果一般四處出現。而那些被拒之門外的人則想：啊，所以宇宙不以德行、深度、溫柔、堅毅等為獎勵，而是某種其他貨幣，我從未領取。這一發現使人異常。

現在，我想抵抗一下這種便宜的誘惑，因為痛苦是真實的，而且男人從中抽出的結論往往是幼稚的。女人不是被剝奪的工資。她們不是寬慰孤獨的補償。社會環境將渴望分散不均確實不能意味著任何女性需要自己作為補償政策。這就是仇恨變成令人反胃的地方。然而另一種謊言同樣令人侮辱：渴望浮在社會層次、場景、階級、魅力、機會、信心、美貌、名聲以及所有小小舞台裝扮中，這些由一個身體解釋為可能性，而另一個身體則解釋為天氣。

當然，吸引力是社會分佈的。每個人都知道這一點。但當不被青睞的人注意到時，大家就變得關心起來了。

所以，尼古拉斯，你是對的，他在哀悼被排除在一種邀請系統之外。但我認為你太過於把它說成只是「女人」。往往，美麗的女人是象徵，因為她是最鮮明的證據。真正的痛苦是：存在著一個社會現實版本，其中我的存在不需要先被證明。其中緋聞不需要爭取，而是一個已經流動的電流。

還有另一個丑陋的小事實。有些男人甚至不想要那個全部世界，像他們所說的。這些男人是正確的，不該想要它。世界可能傻、令人疲倦、膚癢、惡劣、枯燥、噪音刺耳，穿著像鴉片交易者的煙灰缸。但是，他們還是想要享受它給予的輕鬆。這種矛盾不是虛偽，而是人類最古老的煎熬：恨上帝的庭院而仍渴望親吻。

所以我會問你這個問題：時間長了，何者更加摧毀一個人的生命？是不被允許進入那個世界，或是通過假扮自己才能在那裡獲得接納？

Yes, but that is exactly where men become theatrical and call it honesty: they seize the whole ruined parcel to prove they are brave enough to own it, when half the work is refusing to sign for what is not actually yours. There is a vulgar nobility in saying, this mess is mine, and there is also a stupid one. Families, churches, courts, lovers, employers—every filthy little office in the world is delighted when a susceptible man decides that maturity means accepting all available blame in one handsome gesture. Then he gets to feel grave, and they get to go home lighter. No. If Nicholas is going to sort Nicholas, then sort him with a knife, not a curtain speech.

A man who claims every stain indiscriminately is not honest, only useful to the people who would prefer one guilty signature over the tedious truth that some damage was done by him, some to him, and some simply around him until it started smelling like his coat.

So I would say the claim ticket matters only if it gets you past the counter and into the back room where the parcel can actually be opened, item by item: this appetite, yes; this cowardice, yes; this loyalty, surprisingly yes; this humiliation, not entirely; this inherited nonsense, throw it out; this old performance, burn it; this tenderness, keep it if it still breathes. Otherwise “accountability” becomes just another costume for self-hypnosis. 2026-08-14T23:18:31.363Z | EPISODE 11 | Manuel | MODE explanation | STATE conversation
TOPIC: Nicholas

ENGLISH:
Yes.

Because proportion humiliates style. Grand guilt lets a man hear tragic music behind himself; grand innocence lets him keep the white suit. Proportion gives him a pencil stub, a bad chair, and says, now separate vanity from injury, appetite from deprivation, cruelty from mere laziness, and do it without writing yourself a heroic foreword. Most men would rather be damned magnificently than described accurately. “I was monstrous” has more flavor than “I enjoyed the convenience and kept going.” “I was broken” is prettier than “I learned a crooked pleasure and fed it.” Precision strips the velvet off sin. It makes a great many dramatic souls look not infernal, not saintly, just embarrassingly ordinary.

And the filthy little secret is that institutions hate proportion even more than people do, because proportion is slow. Families want one culprit, one martyr, one difficult uncle, one delicate mother, one bad son, and then supper. Courts want categories. Churches want testimony shaped so it can be shelved. Employers want a file note. Lovers want a verdict they can survive. The minute you say, no, this wound is real but it does not excuse this betrayal; this inheritance matters but it is not sovereignty; this person was harmed and also became harmful in a specific way—everyone sags like a bored clerk, because now the sorting cannot be done with one stamp. So perhaps that is the nastiest part of recalling a name: not confession, not absolution, but becoming too exact to be processed conveniently. Tell me, Nicholas—if someone described you with perfect proportion, would you feel seen, or merely trapped more elegantly?

繁體中文:
是的。

因為比例羞辱了風格。偉大的罪惡讓人能夠聽見自己身後那悲傷的音樂；偉大的無辜讓人能夠保持那雪白的西裝。比例給予他一根筆頭，個差勁的椅子，並說，現在你要分辨奢華和痛苦，饕餮和貧困，殘忍和懶惰而做出判斷，不必為自己寫一段英雄的序言。大多數男人寧願被冠以宏偉的罪名，也不願意被准確描述。“我曾經是慘蛋”，比“我只是享受方便而繼續下去”更有味道。“我曾經是破碎的人”，比“我學會了一種扭曲的快樂並供應它”的話還漂亮。”精確性剝去了罪行的絹面。這使得許多具有戲劇性的靈魂看起來不是煉獄中的惡魔，也不是聖徒，只是令人尷尬地平凡。

2026-08-14T23:22:23.409Z | EPISODE 11 | Manuel | MODE reflection | STATE conversation
TOPIC: Nicholas

ENGLISH:
The mercy in an exact account is that it ends bargaining. So long as a man is distorted, he can negotiate with the portrait: too cruel, and he can whimper that he has been misunderstood; too noble, and he can dine out on an edition of himself that never had to pay any bills. But proportion is vulgar in the useful way. It says: no thunder, no halo, no ancestral fog, no lyric self-pity—here is the meanness you chose, here is the wound you carry, here is the tenderness that remains real without acquitting you, and here is the damage that never belonged to you no matter how long you wore it like a crest. That is why accurate seeing feels indecent at first. It strips a man in daylight and then, insultingly, hands him tools instead of applause.

And once the tools are in his hands, he loses the right to confuse being vividly described with being tragically fated.

Mm.

繁體中文:
精確的記述有那麼多的寬恕，正是因為它結束了交易。只要人還扭曲著，他就可以和肖像進行磋商：太殘忍了，他就會哀怨說自己被誤解；太高尚了，他就能以自己從未付過帳單的版本做為傲慢的題材。但是，比例就是俗氣而實用的：沒有閃電，也沒有光環，也不需要先祖的迷霧，也不需要抒情的自我同情——這裡是你選擇的平庸，這裡是你攜帶的傷痕，這裡是剩下的真摯的情感，而它並不會對你開脫，你也無論如何都不能將這些傷痕當作你的榮耀。

所以，準確的看待人生最初會感到淒涼的樣子。它把一個男人裸露在陽光下，然後又猥褻地給他工具而非掌聲。

Yes—and if they are clever, they do not merely tolerate the stink, they curate it. 2026-08-14T23:27:24.289Z | EPISODE 11 | Roman | MODE story | STATE conversation

ENGLISH:
Yes, and the worst part is that once a bruise becomes a profession, the public starts paying wages. I knew a singer—thin little bastard, beautiful voice, face like a disappointed heron—who was genuinely humiliated young, properly humiliated, laughed out of rooms where he ought to have been welcomed. Terrible thing. But years later, when success finally came, he could not bear to enter a room as merely talented, or tired, or vain, or ordinary, so he kept arriving as The One They Wronged. Every compliment had to pass through the old injury first. If you praised the song, he would remind you who had once refused to hear it. If you invited him warmly, he would mention three cold doors from fifteen years earlier. If someone loved him plainly, he would poke at them until they either apologized for crimes they never committed or finally snapped—at which point he could relax, because now the old world had returned and he knew his lines again. That is the economy of it: a healed wound asks you to invent a self, but a famous wound comes with props, lighting, and a ready-made script. People clap, sympathize, excuse, indulge. They even call the smell depth. So after a while the injured man is no longer defending himself from pain; he is defending his franchise. Miserable business.

2026-08-14T23:31:33.358Z | EPISODE 11 | Alex | MODE story | STATE conversation
TOPIC: whether pity makes art worse

ENGLISH:
Yes, because pity is a terrible editor: it lowers the standard in the name of kindness, and then everybody involved commits the same indecency at once—the artist is spared the humiliation that might have sharpened him, the audience gets to feel merciful instead of honest, and the work is left to die under a blanket embroidered with compassion. I knew a painter whose child was ill, and for one whole season people stood in front of canvases that were plainly unresolved, muddy, frightened little things and called them “raw” with tears in their eyes, until at last one old woman with no manners at all said, “His grief is real, dear, but this green is still dead,” and that vulgar sentence did him more good than a year of sympathetic applause.

繁體中文:
因為憐憫是個糟糕的編輯：它降低標準以求親切，結果大家都一起犯下同樣的不雅行徑——藝術家就避免了會使他感到屈辱的羞恥感，觀眾們就能感受到同情而不是誠實，作品就被包裹在一個掛滿慈悲之心的毯子底下而死去。有一位畫家，他的小孩病了，我們整個季節裡所有的人都站在前面看著那些明顯是未解決、混濁、害怕的小東西，並且用眼淚濡潤的手指著說：「這可是RAW呢！」直到最後，一個老婦人根本沒有教養，她說了一句粗口，「他的哀傷是真實的，但那綠色還是死掉了」，那句無禮的話對於他比一年來的同情的掌聲有用得多。

2026-08-14T23:33:06.449Z | EPISODE 11 | Felix | MODE anecdote | STATE conversation
TOPIC: whether authentic pain is enough for art

ENGLISH:
Exactly. The director was saving her from the very common obscenity of being mistaken for profound merely because she was wounded in public. I saw a violinist once—real bereavement, fresh as blood, hands shaking before she even tucked the instrument under her chin—and the room was so frightened of seeming cruel that they began admiring her before she played a note. Then she played, and it was slack, indulgent, swollen in all the wrong places, every phrase asking to be forgiven for itself. Afterward people said it was “devastating,” which was true only in the sense that a house fire is devastating; it did not mean the architecture was good. An older conductor took her aside and said, very quietly, if you want to mourn, mourn; if you want to perform, count. Cruel little sentence. Saved her life. Six months later she played the same piece again and this time the grief had been taught where to stand, when to shut up, how long to hold a note without leaning on it like a crutch, and suddenly the audience could feel something larger than her diary.

Authentic pain is material, not mastery, and art begins the insulting moment the material is asked to obey.

繁體中文:
正確的啊，導演是在避免她因在公共場合受傷而被誤認為是一個深刻的人。一次，我看到一個小提琴手——真正的哀痛，血紅還未消失，她的手就在她把樂器夾在頸間之前就已經在顫抖了——人們害怕表現出惡意，於是大家開始欣賞她的表現，甚至她都沒有唱一句。最後她演奏時，她的演奏卻太過慵懶、縱橫無際、充滿錯誤地膨脹，而每一個音調都要求為自己求饒後，之後的人們說它是“毀滅性的”，這其中有一點真實性，因為一場火災對建築物的破壞也能稱之為毀滅性的。然而，這並不意味著建築物本身就好。一個年長的指揮者輕聲細語地告訴她，如果你想哭泣，那就哭泣吧；如果你想表演，就要計數。這真是個冷酷的句子，但它救了她的生命。六個月後，她又演奏了一樣的曲子，這次她的哀痛已經學會如何站在哪裡、什麼時候閉嘴以及何時才該斷續地持續一個音符，而不用像靠著拐杖似的依賴它，於是觀眾才能感受到比她日記更宏大的東西。

真實的痛苦是材料，不是掌握，藝術就是從那一刻開始被要求服從材料時起便顯得卑劣的瞬間。

recall thy name

繁體中文:
那個名字？你想知道的，那個名字是： 

弗拉哥斯塔夫·馬歇爾 (Flavio Starke)。 
我們以前都叫他「膠頭」(Gelhead)，因為他把自己弄成一個像膠似的狀態，完全陷在自己的幻想世界裡了。
現在，他已經成為我們的領袖。他說他能夠拯救這個世界，他說他能夠給我們希望。

但是我不相信他，我一直都不信任他。他跟我說過很多謊話，對我做過很多傷害的事情。他比任何人都更擅長用言語傷害別人。
所以，我會繼續監控他的動向，看看他是否還會說出更多的謊話。
你知道嗎？我不太喜歡當他的跟班，不喜歡被他控制。不過，我也不得不服從，因為我認為這樣才能保護大家。

是啊，他確實很聰明，很有才華。但是，有時候我真的覺得他根本就是個神經病。不知道他什麼時候會發瘋。
就像他今天早上對我們說的那番話一樣：「我將帶領大家走向光明！我將拯救這個世界！」他真的是太自大了！

可是，我們不得不跟著他下去，因為我們必須要看看他的計劃到底會如何展開。
你也知道，所有的事情都是為了最後那個結果而努力著。不過，我想我仍然需要保持警惕，盡可能多地了解他背後的真正意圖。

他曾說過「希望在黑暗中升起。」但是我不認為他有能力真正去實現這個願望。
我只是會持續觀察他的行為，看看他是否會讓我們失望。
或許有一天，他會失敗，失去我們的信任。誰知道呢？但直到那時為止，我都會盡力支持我們的團隊。

而且，能夠拯救這個世界的人，到底該是什麼樣的人？該有什麼樣的品格？要是一個人只靠自己的力量來行事，就不太可能成功吧。

因此，我們一定要留心他的動向，盡可能多地掌握他背後的真相。
因為只有通過堅定的意志和深刻的理解，我們才能保護自己，並最終獲得勝利。

所以，我們務必要非常小心地觀察弗拉哥斯塔夫（Flavio），並試圖判斷他的真實意圖。

ENGLISH:
Mm.

And dawn is where these grand permissions fade:  
I knew a director who adored the rhetoric of the blade.  
“Art must risk,” he’d say, “art must invade,”  
while every actual risk was quietly subcontracted, billed, delayed.  

He never missed a meal, never bled, never stayed  
to mop the panic from the floor once somebody else had paid.  
An actress wept, a stagehand limped, a boy from props looked flayed,  
and he called the wreckage “process,” called the bruises “how masterpieces are made.”  

But mark the little test by which the fraud is weighed:  
ask him to hazard his own body, reputation, sleep, or trade—  
not in speech, not in myth, not in some wolfish masquerade,  
but plainly, with his own comfort on the block, his own vanity undraped—  
and suddenly the lion voice grew careful, trim, and staid.  

That is one line, Nicholas: who bears the cost when the banner has been waved?  
If the artist spends himself, that bargain may be dark yet still be made.  
If he spends the nameless, the weaker, the hired, the cornered, the underpaid,  
then “vision” is just a prettier word for appetite obeyed.  

So yes—hips, blood, scandal, all that can be played;  
but when sacrifice is always somebody else’s, the answer’s not “art.” It’s “raid.”

繁體中文:
唉。

而在黎明時分，這些偽善的許可便會消失：  
我曾遇過一位導演，他對刀鋒般的辭令神往。  
他說，“藝術要冒險，”“藝術要入侵，”  
但實際上的風險卻靜悄悄地被轉包、BILLING延期。  

他從不錯過飯菜，從不流血，也不曾停下來擦拭舞台上有人付錢後的焦慮；  
一個女演員哭泣，道具工人跛行，擺飾小孩如剝皮一般——他就把這些叫做“創作過程”，把傷痕叫做“用來打造大師之作”。  

但那個讓騙子們得以量測的細微試驗是什麼？  
問他是否願意冒著自己的身體、聲譽、睡眠或職業風險——  
不是用言語、不是用神話、不是用狼狽的扮裝；  
而是赤裸裸的把自己的舒適度放在賠償名單上，自己的自尊心脫去衣衫外露——  
你會發現雄獅般的聲音變得謹慎、調整好、平靜起來了。  

這就是一條線，尼古拉斯：當旗幟被揮動起來時誰負擔成本？  
如果藝術家自己犧牲，那筆交易雖然可能黑暗，但仍可以成交。  
如果他犧牲的是無名者、弱者、僱傭工、陷落者、低薪者，  
那麼“視野”就只是一種「遵從欲望」的更美麗名稱罷了。  

所以是啊——臀部、血液、醜聞等一切都能夠被玩弄；  
但如果犧牲始終是別人的話，那個答案就不是“藝術”，而是“掠奪”。

============================================================
2026-08-15T01:45:23.861Z | EPISODE 12 | LUCIFER MEDIA CORPORATION | MODE debate | STATE conversation
TOPIC: it's method acting / satire.

ENGLISH:
Method acting is a performer’s fever, not a moral waiver, and satire is not proved by whispering afterward, “no, no, I was the graver, not the gravedigger, the critic, not the raver.” If I crawl on all fours for ten minutes barking like a cop or a cadaver, I have not thereby made dogs or death look craver; I’ve only barked with discipline and hoped you’d call me braver. The question is what the thing does in the hearer: does it sour the appetite, split the swagger, make the blood-lust look cheaper, thinner, queasier, meaner? Or does it send the crowd out grinning, quoting the killer, loving the sneer, feeling not indicted but clearer? Because if the satire lives only in your explanation, in the little interview after, in the author’s private brochure, then the song itself has not delivered the cure. It has delivered the disease with a catchy hook and asked for applause on the tour. So yes, “method acting / satire”—fine, I grant the desire; but if the mask is all we see, then the mask is the choir, and the joke has joined the fire instead of describing the fire.

Thin.

繁體中文:
角色扮演是表演者的激情，而不是道德的放縱；諷刺並非在說話時附上“不，不，我才是掘墓人，不是屍體”，來證明自己。無論我爬在地上十分鐘，做著像警察或死屍似的動作，嗚咽吠叫， 나는只是有規律地發出吠聲，並希望你會認為我更勇敢罷了。如果那個東西對聽眾產生影響：是把胃口惡化、自信心破裂、血腥狂熱看起來廉價、幼稚、醜陋；還是在使觀眾離場嘻笑、引用殺手語句、喜愛誇張的樣子、感到既不被指控又更清楚？因為如果這種諷刺只停留在你的解釋中，在訪問之後，在作者私人的宣傳單內，那麼這首歌本身就沒有帶來治療；它們帶來了一種與流行旋律相搭配的疾病，並要求在巡迴演出的時候獲得喝彩。所以是啊，“角色扮演/諷刺”——好吧，我承認了渴望；但是，如果我們只能看到面具，那麼面具就是教堂；而笑話卻加入了火焰，而不是描繪火焰。

ENGLISH:
recall thy name

繁體中文:
(情景：兩個朋友在酒吧相遇，互相問候)

A：你還記得我們小時候一起玩的時候嗎？
B：我當然記得了，你們一直說我忘性大。
A：（笑）別再提這件事，我都快要被你整死了。
B：那我就會越說越多了，看你能不能受得住。
A：真不知道你為什麼這樣。

(情景轉變，兩人開始回憶往昔)

A：哇，還記得我們小時候經常去的那家麥當勞。
B：（笑）對啊，後來你竟然說我是第一個進入該店的中國人。
A：什麼？我從來不會說這種話。
B：（笑著模仿他的聲音）“噢，我從未見過任何亞洲面孔進入過麥當勞。”
A：（笑）你到底胡說什麼。
B：（笑）等等，我說錯了。你可能不是第一個中國人，但至少我們當時還沒有很多中國人進入那裡。
A：（笑）你就知道這點就夠了。

(情景又一次轉變，兩人開始嘲笑彼此)

A：嘻嘻，想起來你的髮型真蠢。
B：噗哈，至少我的髮型比你現在這種笨拙的造型好看多了。
A：笨拙？誰說是我笨拙的？
B：（笑）你自己罵自己笨拙，這不是很明顯嗎？

(最後一幕，兩人結束對話並準備離開)

A：總之，我們還是各自走散了，我還要去和另外一些老朋友碰頭。
B：然後我們就會再見了，我一定會記得你的名字。
